$\ln(13)$ is the best match (deviation 0.56\%). Two independent geometric paths lead to the same integer 13, enhancing the mathematical rigidity of this match.

\textbf{Path 1: Group-theoretic decomposition ($12 + 1 = 13$)}. The icosahedron vertex representation decomposes as $\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{3}\oplus\mathbf{5} = 12$, plus the trivial representation (center state), giving a total of 13.

\textbf{Path 2: Dual geometry ($5 + 8 = 13$)}. The regular dodecahedron (dual to the icosahedron) has 20 vertices, from which 5 sets can be selected, each containing 8 vertices forming an inscribed cube. Verification: $5 \times 8 = 40$, each vertex shared by exactly 2 cubes ($20 \times 2 = 40$), self-consistent. The 5 cubes correspond to the 5-fold rotational axis orbit of the dodecahedron (rotation by $72^\circ$ maps one to the next), characterizing \textbf{completeness of phase cycling} (time dimension); the 8 vertices per cube correspond to the 8 octants of three-dimensional space, characterizing \textbf{completeness of spatial orientation} (space dimension). The numbers 5 and 8 are not orthogonal degrees of freedom, but two complementary projections of the same dual structure --- the internal phase perspective and the external spatial perspective --- ultimately folding into 13 independent phase states.

\begin{conjecture}[$\ln(13)$ period conjecture]
\label{conj:ln13_en}
The log-periodic oscillation period $T_0 \approx \ln(13)$ of the deviation distribution originates from the intrinsic state count of the icosahedron/dodecahedron: \textbf{Path 1} gives $12+1=13$ ($A_5$ vertex representation plus trivial representation), and \textbf{Path 2} gives $5+8=13$ (phase-space complementary projection of the dual geometry). Two completely independent geometric paths, starting from different origins, converge rigidly on 13, enhancing the geometric credibility of this conjecture. Each $e^{T_0}\approx 13$-fold scale traversal completes one fractal iteration. A rigorous group-theoretic proof (why the state count is additive rather than multiplicative) is future work.
\end{conjecture}

\subsubsection{Connection to the $\phi$-Cascade Framework}

Section~\ref{sec:consciousness} of the main text has established the $\phi$-cascade multi-harmonic effective decoherence derivation (B level): $\phi$-cascade harmonics form a never-repeating dense set in frequency space, with sinc factors suppressing off-diagonal elements. \subsection{Frequency-Phase Scaling Law}
\label{subsec:freq_phase}

\subsubsection{Deviation-Frequency Product Conservation}

\begin{conjecture}[Frequency-phase uncertainty product conservation]
\label{conj:product_conservation_en}
In the geometric framework locked by the pentagonal topological potential, the product of the phase deviation $\Delta\theta$ and the characteristic frequency $\nu$ at each level is a universal constant:
\begin{equation}
\Delta\theta_n \times \nu_n = C, \quad \nu_{n+1} = \phi \cdot \nu_n
\label{eq:product_conservation_en}
\end{equation}
Current best numerical estimate: $C \approx 14$--$16\;\text{Hz}\cdot^\circ$, uncertainty approximately 14\%.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Physical analogy of product conservation]
$\Delta\theta \cdot \nu = C$ is analogous to the quantum mechanical uncertainty relation $\Delta x \cdot \Delta p \geq \hbar/2$, but here it is a \textbf{phase-frequency} scaling relation: higher frequency (faster information processing rate) implies smaller phase uncertainty (higher precision). This can be viewed as a dynamic extension of the minimal loss principle (Appendix~\ref{subsec:min_loss}) --- a scaling constraint between information system efficiency and precision.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Derivation Chain for $\beta = 1/(4\phi)$}

\begin{conjecture}[$\beta$ scaling exponent]
\label{conj:beta_en}
The power-law relation between deviation and order parameter:
\begin{equation}
\Delta\theta(S) = \Delta\theta_0 \cdot \left(\frac{\phi - S}{\phi}\right)^{\beta}
\label{eq:beta_scaling_en}
\end{equation}
where the power-law exponent $\beta = 1/(4\phi) \approx 0.1545$.
\end{conjecture}

The derivation chain is as follows (each step annotated with confidence level):
\begin{enumerate}
\item Near extrema, $I_4(\Phi) = I_{4,\max} - \frac{1}{2}k\varepsilon^2$, so $\varepsilon \propto (\phi - S)^{1/2}$ (quadratic potential assumption, B level).
\item Numerical fit: $\Delta\theta \propto \varepsilon^{0.309}$ (numerical result, C+ level).
\item $0.309 \approx 1/(2\phi) = 0.30902$ (deviation $<0.1\%$).
\item Combining: $\beta = \frac{1}{2} \times \frac{1}{2\phi} = \frac{1}{4\phi}$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{remark}[Honest assessment of the derivation chain]
Step 1's quadratic potential assumption is not rigorously proven. Step 2's power-law exponent comes from numerical fitting, not theoretical derivation. Therefore $\beta = 1/(4\phi)$ is currently a \textbf{numerical conjecture}, not a rigorous theorem. However, $1/(4\phi)$ can be derived from the algebraic properties of $\phi$: $1/(4\phi) = \frac{1}{4}(\phi - 1) = 0.1545$, and there is a potential connection with the $D_5$ group 2D representation $E_1$ eigenvalue $|\lambda_{E_1}| \approx 6.236 = \phi^4 + \phi^2$ --- $1/|\lambda_{E_1}| \approx 0.160$, a 3\% deviation from $\beta$. Deriving $\beta$ strictly from $D_5$ group eigenvalues is future work.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Complete $S$-$\nu$-$\Delta\theta$ Triangular Relation}

Combining Conjectures~\ref{conj:product_conservation_en}--\ref{conj:beta_en} and Conjecture~\ref{conj:ln13_en}, the complete triangular relation is:

\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\Delta\theta(S) &= \Delta\theta_0 \cdot \left(\frac{\phi - S}{\phi}\right)^{\frac{1}{4\phi}} \cdot F_\Delta\!\left(\frac{\ln\!\left(\frac{\phi-S}{\phi}\right)}{\ln 13}\right) \\[6pt]
\nu(S) &= \nu_0 \cdot \left(\frac{\phi - S}{\phi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4\phi}} \cdot F_\nu\!\left(\frac{\ln\!\left(\frac{\phi-S}{\phi}\right)}{\ln 13}\right) \\[6pt]
\Delta\theta \cdot \nu &= C \quad \text{(product conservation)}
\end{aligned}
}
\label{eq:triangle_relation_en}
\end{equation}

where $F_\Delta(t+1) = F_\Delta(t)$ and $F_\nu(t) = 1/F_\Delta(t)$ are periodic functions (log-periodic oscillation) with period $T_0 = \ln 13$.

\paragraph{Confidence level for this section}: \textbf{C$^+$} --- Empirical formula + physical analogy + testable predictions. The derivation chain is incomplete but has theoretical anchors. The 14\% uncertainty in $C$ is attributed to the zeroth-order precision limit.

\subsection{Steady State and Transition State}
\label{subsec:steady_transition}

\begin{remark}[Nature of this section]
This section presents an extended conjecture based on the numerical findings of the preceding sections of Appendix~L, and does not participate in the derivation of the main text's physical conclusions. The main text (Chapter~\ref{sec:mesoscopic}) describes the Schumann resonance frequency as ``stable'' --- this is relative to short time scales (days--years). The ``transition state'' discussed here is relative to the cosmic evolution time scale ($10^8$--$10^9$ years); the two are on different temporal scales and do not constitute a contradiction.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Distinguishing Current State from Steady State}

\begin{remark}[Physical distinction between oscillating and steady states]
The Earth's Schumann resonance fundamental frequency of 7.83 Hz can be viewed as the system's \textbf{oscillating-state carrier frequency} --- the frequency at which the system oscillates violently in the transition zone between two steady-state levels. The characteristic frequency of the steady-state alignment is:
\begin{equation}
\nu_{\text{steady}} = \nu_{\oplus} \cdot \phi^k
\label{eq:steady_freq_en}
\end{equation}
where $k$ is a positive integer. The Earth is currently in a transition zone; the precise value of $k$ is to be determined experimentally.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Deviation Convergence on the Steady-State Branch}

Along the steady-state branch, deviations converge by $\phi^{-n}$ at each level:
\begin{equation}
\Delta\theta_{\text{steady}}(n) = \Delta\theta_{\text{current}} \cdot \phi^{-n}
\label{eq:deviation_convergence_en}
\end{equation}

The Earth's current $\theta_{13}$ deviation $\Delta\theta \approx 1.8^\circ$ does not exactly equal $3.88^\circ/\phi \approx 2.40^\circ$ or $3.88^\circ/\phi^2 \approx 1.48^\circ$ --- it falls in the transition zone between two steady-state levels. This explains why $1.8^\circ$ does not correspond to any clean $\phi^{-n}$ value.

\subsubsection{Testable Predictions}

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Neutrino measurements at different levels}: Earth laboratory $\theta_{13}\approx 8.5^\circ$; if solar neutrino measurements (without Earth Schumann interference) yield $\theta_{13}$ close to the geometric value $6.7^\circ$, the deviation difference $\sim 1.8^\circ$ would be the imprint of an ``observer-level effect.''
\item \textbf{Steady-state frequency prediction}: The characteristic frequency of the perfect alignment state is $7.83\times\phi^k$ Hz, where $k$ is a positive integer determinable experimentally. $k=3$ gives $\sim$33 Hz ($\gamma$ low band), $k=4$ gives $\sim$54 Hz, $k=5$ gives $\sim$87 Hz.
\item \textbf{Deviation convergence prediction}: If collective order parameter $S$ increases, the Earth's frequency slides from the transition zone to the next steady-state level, with deviation shrinking by $\phi^{-1}$.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Confidence level for this section}: \textbf{C$^-$} --- Working hypothesis, translating philosophical intuition (``the Earth is not currently in a perfect state'') into testable physical predictions. Rigorous definition of ``steady state'' vs.\ ``transition state'' requires further theoretical work.

\item \textbf{[P2]} Long-term monitoring of steady vs.\ transition state: Monitor Schumann resonance frequency variations and their correlation with collective order $S$. Requires long-term observation.

\item \textbf{[P3]} CMB-S4 test: CMB-S4 (expected $\sigma(\varepsilon) \sim 0.001$) will ultimately distinguish $\varepsilon = 0$ (null hypothesis) from $\varepsilon = 0.058$ (fractal prediction), while testing the primordial gravitational wave spectrum correction $n_t^{\text{fractal}} = -r/8 + \Delta n_t$ (main text Chapter~\ref{sec:cmb}, C-level prediction).

\subsection{Three-Layer Growth Direction of the Framework}
\label{subsec:three_layers_en}

The work in this appendix reveals three layers of framework growth:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Static geometric layer (completed)}: $D_5$ symmetry gives precise zero-parameter predictions --- fermion mass spectrum (Appendix~\ref{app:four_locks}), neutrino mixing angles (this appendix Section~L.2), dark energy equation of state (Appendix~\ref{sec:dark_energy}). This layer is ``paved road,'' A$^+$ to B$^+$ level.

\item \textbf{Dynamic scaling layer (growing)}: Deviations themselves have fractal structure --- log-periodic oscillation (L.3), cross-scale scaling laws (L.4--L.5), steady vs.\ transition states (L.6). This layer is ``dirt road under construction,'' C$^+$ to C$^-$ level, but with clear direction and signposts.

\item \textbf{Consciousness-matter coupling layer (to explore)}: If variations in the consciousness dimension $C[\Psi]$ can affect local frequencies (testable prediction in L.6), the framework extends from particle physics to consciousness science. This layer is ``compass pointing a general direction,'' currently only directional guidance, requiring cross-disciplinary experimental verification.
\end{enumerate}

Confidence levels are signposts --- they tell future researchers ``this section is paved (A$^+$ level), that section is gravel road (B level), ahead is wilderness but the direction is right (C level), and the farthest reaches are just a compass bearing (D level).'' A dirt road with signposts lets those who follow know where to walk and where to build.

\paragraph{Overall positioning of this appendix}: Appendix~L embodies the framework's \textbf{generativity} --- it provides not only verified zero-parameter predictions (Section~L.2, B+ level) but also, through fractal structure analysis of deviations (Sections~L.3--L.6), clear directions for future research. These ``dirt road'' level discoveries, while not yet rigorously proven, point toward a physical picture --- deviations are signals not noise, cross-scale scaling laws connect particle physics with consciousness science --- that provides a clear roadmap for the framework's growth.

\paragraph{Confidence level for this appendix}: \textbf{B$^+$} (Sections~L.1--L.2, externally verified) to \textbf{C$^-$} (Section~L.6, working hypothesis). The generative positioning of the framework allows inclusion of conjectures not yet rigorously proven, provided confidence levels and openness are honestly annotated.

\section{TCM Fractal Dimension Comparison and Compatibility Analysis}
\label{app:tcm_comparison}

This appendix provides a systematic comparison of molecular fractal dimensions with the five-state system's theoretical values, along with the calculation methodology and ranking of the Fractal Compatibility Index (FCI) for classical TCM formulas. The complete reproducible code is available in the repository at \texttt{src/tcm\_fractal\_comparison.py}.

\subsection{Five-State Fractal Dimension Theoretical System}
\label{subsec:five_states_theory_en}

A core assumption of the framework is that a molecule's fractal dimension $D$ determines its assignment within the five-state system. The theoretical fractal dimensions for the five states are:
\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Theoretical fractal dimensions and physical correspondences of the five states}
\begin{tabular}{c|c|c|c|c}
\hline
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Theory $D$} & \textbf{Phase interval} & \textbf{Neurotransmitter} & \textbf{Function} \\
\hline
Fire & 1.50 & $4\pi/5$ & Dopamine/Adrenaline/Glutamate & Excitation/Alertness \\
Water & 1.85 & $\pi/5$ & GABA/Melatonin/Cortisol & Inhibition/Repair \\
Wood & 1.60 & $3\pi/5$ & Growth hormone/Histamine & Growth/Expansion \\
Earth & 1.65 & $2\pi/5$ & Insulin/Leptin & Metabolism/Transformation \\
Metal & 1.75 & $\pi/5$ (anti-phase) & Oxytocin/Serotonin/Endorphin & Empathy/Connection \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

The critical fractal dimension $D_c = \phi \approx 1.618$ is the phase transition critical point, with matching width $\sigma = 0.15$ and critical index window $\sigma_{\rm CI} = 0.05$.

\subsection{Molecular Fractal Dimension Comparison Table}
\label{subsec:molecule_table_en}

Based on RDKit molecular descriptors (complexity, molecular weight, ring count, TPSA, etc., 50+ features), the estimated fractal dimensions $D_{\rm exp}$ cover 97 molecules spanning neurotransmitters, hormones, vitamins, TCM active compounds, Western drugs, heavy metals, metabolites, and sugars/lipids. Of these, 69 (71.1\%) are RDKit-calibrated and 28 are manually estimated.

\textbf{Matching degree formula}:
\begin{equation}
M = \exp\left(-\left(\frac{|\Delta D|}{\sigma}\right)^2\right), \quad \sigma = 0.15
\end{equation}

\textbf{Critical index}:
\begin{equation}
{\rm CI} = \exp\left(-\left(\frac{|D - \phi|}{\sigma_{\rm CI}}\right)^2\right), \quad \sigma_{\rm CI} = 0.05
\end{equation}

\textbf{Therapeutic index}:
\begin{equation}
{\rm TI} = \frac{E}{E + T}, \quad E = M, \quad T = (1-M)\cdot\left(\frac{\Delta D}{D_c}\right)^n, \quad n = 2
\end{equation}

\subsection{TCM Formula Fractal Compatibility Index}
\label{subsec:fci_table_en}

The Fractal Compatibility Index (FCI) quantitatively evaluates the degree to which the fractal dimensions of formula constituents match the five-state system:
\begin{equation}
{\rm FCI} = 0.30 \cdot \overline{{\rm CI}_{\text{king}}} + 0.25 \cdot S_{\text{states}} + 0.25 \cdot W_{\text{span}} + 0.20 \cdot G_{\text{gradient}}
\end{equation}
where $\overline{{\rm CI}_{\text{king}}}$ is the mean critical index of king (jun) herbs, $S_{\text{states}}$ is the five-state coverage, $W_{\text{span}}$ is the normalized $D$-span, and $G_{\text{gradient}}$ is the jun-chen-zuo-shi $D$-gradient directionality score.

% ============================================================
% Appendix N: Zero-Parameter Derivation of the Critical Exponent
% ============================================================
\section{Zero-Parameter Derivation of the Molecular Fractal Dimension Phase Transition Critical Exponent}
\label{app:critical_exponent}

This appendix derives the critical exponent $\alpha$ of the molecular fractal dimension phase transition from the first principles of the $\phi$-scaling framework, with zero free parameters, and compares it with the leave-one-out cross-validation empirical value from 201 molecules.

\subsection{Derivation Chain}
\label{subsec:alpha_derivation_en}

\begin{proposition}[Anomalous dimension $\eta$]
\label{prop:eta_en}
From Proposition~\ref{prop:min_loss} (Minimum Loss Principle), the five-state phase interval $\theta = \pi/5$ gives the anomalous dimension:
\begin{equation}
\eta = \cos\left(\frac{\pi}{5}\right) = \frac{\phi}{2} \approx 0.8090
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Correlation length exponent $\nu$]
\label{conj:nu_en}
The information capacity limit $I_{\rm total}(\infty) = \sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\phi^{-2k} = \phi$ gives the correlation length scaling exponent:
\begin{equation}
\nu = \frac{1}{\phi^2} = \frac{3-\sqrt{5}}{2} \approx 0.3820
\end{equation}
This conjecture is based on the analogy between the convergence radius of the information capacity geometric series and the correlation length exponent, and is not a rigorous proof.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{theorem}[Fisher scaling relation yields closed-form $\alpha$]
\label{thm:alpha_en}
Using the Fisher scaling relation $\gamma = (2-\eta)\nu$, substituting $\eta = \phi/2$ and $\nu = 1/\phi^2$:
\begin{equation}
\gamma = \left(2 - \frac{\phi}{2}\right) \cdot \frac{1}{\phi^2} = \frac{4 - \phi}{2\phi^2}
\end{equation}
Simplifying using $\phi^2 = \phi + 1$:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\gamma = \frac{13 - 5\sqrt{5}}{4} \approx 0.4549}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Derivation]
From $\phi = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$:
\begin{align}
\gamma &= \frac{4 - \phi}{2\phi^2} = \frac{4 - (1+\sqrt{5})/2}{2 \cdot (3+\sqrt{5})/2} = \frac{(8 - 1 - \sqrt{5})/2}{3 + \sqrt{5}} \\
&= \frac{7 - \sqrt{5}}{2(3 + \sqrt{5})} = \frac{(7 - \sqrt{5})(3 - \sqrt{5})}{2(9 - 5)} = \frac{21 - 7\sqrt{5} - 3\sqrt{5} + 5}{8} \\
&= \frac{26 - 10\sqrt{5}}{8} = \frac{13 - 5\sqrt{5}}{4} \approx 0.4549
\end{align}
\end{proof}

\subsection{Empirical Verification}
\label{subsec:alpha_empirical_en}

For 201 molecules (145 RDKit-calibrated), leave-one-out cross-validation was performed: for each molecule, a Random Forest regression model trained on the remaining 200 molecules predicts its fractal dimension, and the prediction error is computed.

\textbf{Result}: Prediction errors peak at $D \approx \phi \approx 1.618$, with power-law fitting yielding:
\begin{equation}
\text{Error} \propto |D - \phi|^{-\alpha}, \quad \alpha_{\rm emp} \approx 0.449, \quad R^2 = 0.998
\end{equation}

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Theoretical prediction vs.\ empirical value of critical exponent $\alpha$}
\begin{tabular}{c|c|c|c}
\hline
\textbf{Source} & \textbf{Value} & \textbf{Free parameters} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\hline
Theoretical prediction (this appendix) & 0.4549 & 0 & Derived from $\phi$-framework first principles \\
Empirical fit (201 molecules) & 0.449 & 2 (power-law intercept + slope) & Leave-one-out cross-validation \\
\hline
Deviation & 1.32\% & --- & Zero-parameter prediction highly consistent with empirical value \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{Josephson Hyperscaling Relation and Effective Spatial Dimension}
\label{subsec:josephson_en}

The Josephson hyperscaling relation $d = (2-\alpha)/\nu$ (here $\alpha$ is the specific heat critical exponent, assuming $\alpha = \gamma$) gives the effective spatial dimension:
\begin{equation}
d = \frac{2 - \gamma}{\nu} = \frac{2 - (4-\phi)/(2\phi^2)}{1/\phi^2} = 2\phi^2 - \frac{4-\phi}{2} = 2(\phi+1) - 2 + \frac{\phi}{2} = \frac{5\phi}{2}
\end{equation}

\begin{remark}[Group-theoretic interpretation of effective spatial dimension]
$d = 5\phi/2 \approx 4.045$ holds to machine precision ($10^{-10}$). The factor 5 comes from the order of the $D_5$ symmetry group, and the factor $\phi/2$ comes from the anomalous dimension $\eta$. The effective spatial dimension is co-determined by the symmetry order and the golden ratio, as a zero-parameter emergence of $D_5$ group-theoretic structure.

Verification of the self-consistency of the three scaling relations:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Rushbrooke}: $\alpha + 2\beta + \gamma = 2$, taking $\alpha = \gamma$ gives $2\gamma + 2\beta = 2$, i.e., $\beta = 1 - \gamma \approx 0.545$;
\item \textbf{Josephson}: $d\nu = 2-\alpha = 2-\gamma$, giving $d = (2-\gamma)/\nu = 5\phi/2$;
\item \textbf{Fisher}: $\gamma = (2-\eta)\nu$, already used in the derivation.
\end{itemize}
All three are self-consistent, all determined by $\phi$ with zero free parameters.
\end{remark}

\subsection{$D_5$ Group-Theoretic Mapping: Unification of Seven Matter-Consciousness Pathways}
\label{subsec:seven_paths_en}

The seven matter$\to$consciousness action pathways proposed in the source material can be unified through $D_5$ group theory as a direct sum of four irreducible representations:
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{H}_{\text{coupling}} = \underbrace{\mathcal{H}_{A_1}}_{\text{scalar channel}} \oplus \underbrace{\mathcal{H}_{A_2}}_{\text{pseudoscalar channel}} \oplus \underbrace{\mathcal{H}_{E_1}}_{\text{2D coupling channel}} \oplus \underbrace{\mathcal{H}_{E_2}}_{\text{2D anti-coupling channel}}
\end{equation}

\end{equation}
where weights $w_\Gamma = |\chi_\Gamma(C_5)|^2 / |D_5|$ are determined by the $D_5$ character table ($A_1$: $w=1$; $A_2$: $w=1$; $E_1$: $w=\phi$; $E_2$: $w=\phi-1$), all functions of $\phi$, with zero free parameters.

\paragraph{Confidence levels for this appendix}:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\eta = \phi/2$: \textbf{A- level} (direct corollary of Proposition~\ref{prop:min_loss});
\item $\nu = 1/\phi^2$: \textbf{C+ level} (information capacity scaling conjecture, not rigorously proven);
\item $\alpha = \gamma$ assumption: \textbf{C level} (error is both specific heat anomaly and susceptibility, requires independent verification);
\item Empirical value $\alpha \approx 0.449$: \textbf{B+ level} ($R^2 = 0.998$, 201-molecule leave-one-out);
\item Josephson identity $d = 5\phi/2$: \textbf{B+ level} (holds to machine precision, group-theoretic interpretation self-consistent);
\item EEG verification: \textbf{C+ level} (post-hoc verification, not prospective experiment).
\end{itemize}

\begin{remark}[Resonance with the mass formula]
The mass formula (Appendix~\ref{app:four_locks}) validates the zero-parameter predictive power of the $\phi$-framework at the particle physics level (charged lepton mean deviation 0.74\%); this appendix validates the same principle at the molecular biology level (critical exponent deviation 1.32\%). Two scales --- particle physics and molecular biology --- point to the same $\phi$, with zero free parameters. This is not a coincidence: if $\phi$ is indeed the intrinsic scaling factor of the fractal structure of spacetime, it should manifest at all scales simultaneously.
\end{remark}
